## Summary
'Safe Homes' (al-Buyut al-Amina) is a weekly programme by Omar Abdelkafy on the Dubai channel, in forty-seven episodes, on building the Muslim home from good choice and engagement through married life and the raising of children, with viewers' phone-in questions throughout. It is a guidance programme and not a fatwa programme, and the lecturer's views on disputed matters are recorded as his views. This page covers the first episodes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:32:00}

## Episode 1: good choice
- He announces a new weekly programme on the Dubai channel called 'Safe Homes'. Each week he will take one topic on the Muslim home, from its founding through married life to sons and daughters, sometimes with specialists in family relations (sociologists, psychologists, educators), and then take questions from viewers by phone. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:01:30}
- In his preface he says a society is safe only when its homes are safe, and that homes without firm faith are easily shaken by storms. He says the West suffers from family breakdown, while the Muslim family still stands, and he describes himself as an optimist. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:04:00}
- The topic of the first episode is good choice in building the Muslim home. He says many people bite their fingers in regret after twenty or thirty years of marriage, and he voices the families' objection: we asked about the young man and found him praying in the mosque, then discovered after the wedding that he was stingy, tied to his mother or unable to decide. He promises to treat this from the Qur'an and the Sunna. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:05:30}
- He explains that the word 'zawj' in Arabic is used for a man and for a woman, and means one who needs his like. He holds that the Qur'an uses the word for a spouse where the two are alike in righteousness, such as [[person/adam|Adam]] and his wife, Zakariya and his wife, and the people of Paradise and their spouses. Where faith differs it says 'wife of' instead: the wives of [[person/nuh|Nuh]] and [[person/lut|Lut]], despite their prophethood, and [[person/asiya|Asiya]], the righteous wife of [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]. This is the lecturer's own inference, not quoted from a commentator, and rests on [[passage/quran-66-10|verse 10 of Surat al-Tahrim]] and the verse after it. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:08:30}
- He states that a person chooses only with his limited senses and then asks guidance through [[practice/istikhara|istikhara]], and that young people are sometimes dazzled by appearances. He mentions that the Prophet advised looking at the woman one intends to marry (the wording is not typed here). He warns young men raised outside the mosque against being swayed by looks. (needs_source: part of this passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:10:00}
- He says God chose [[person/hawwa|Hawwa]] for [[person/adam|Adam]]. Adam was made from the earth, so men bear what hard earth bears, while Hawwa was made from a living being and is marked by tenderness, so a mother weeps when her son returns, when he leaves, when he succeeds and when her daughter marries. He adds that God put both a material and an emotional side in each, but that a woman's emotion comes first. This is his own reading and is not cited to a chain of transmission. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:11:30}
- He reports that scholars agree a woman needs about five kind words a day from the men in her family, roughly one with each prayer, and urges men to give them instead of troubling her. He names no scholar for this claimed consensus. (needs_source: the consensus attributed to unnamed scholars) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:12:30}
- He sees man and woman as complementary models, not mirror images, and criticises the talk of 'women's rights' and 'men's rights' as if a marriage were a company with 49 and 51 shares. He wants homes founded on grace (fadl) rather than strict justice alone, pointing to the Qur'anic reminder not to forget grace between spouses in [[passage/quran-2-237|verse 237 of Surat al-Baqara]], and says homes built on bare justice can collapse. He promises to return to this in later episodes. See [[concept/adl-wal-fadl|justice and grace]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:15:00}
- He outlines the [[practice/istikhara|istikhara prayer]] and its supplication, and says it is the highest level of good choosing because the One who chooses is the One who knows the unseen. The wording of the supplication is not typed here. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:15:30}
- He retells the story of [[person/musa|Musa]] in Madyan: he fled Egypt after killing the Egyptian, watered the flock of the two daughters of a righteous man, and one of them came 'walking with modesty'. He tells his female viewers that [[concept/haya|modesty]] is faith and that when it is lost, safety is lost. He then notes that one daughter proposed hiring him because he was strong and trustworthy, and that her father offered one of his daughters in marriage in return for eight years of work, ten if Musa chose. He presents this as an example of God choosing the spouses. See verses [[passage/quran-28-23|23]], [[passage/quran-28-25|25]], [[passage/quran-28-26|26]] and [[passage/quran-28-27|27]] of Surat al-Qasas. The window at 00:18:30 is a repetition loop. (needs_source: the wife's name mentioned in the passage is unclear and is not written) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:19:30}
- He says the Prophet's own marriages were also by God's choice: in the case of [[person/zaynab-bint-jahsh|Zaynab bint Jahsh]], after [[person/zayd-ibn-haritha|Zayd]] had ended his marriage to her, the Qur'an says in [[passage/quran-33-37|verse 37 of Surat al-Ahzab]] that God married her to him, and he mentions the marriage to [[person/aisha|Aisha]] and a report that an angel showed her to the Prophet. He adds that [[person/khadija|Khadija]] chose the Prophet after her servant described what he had seen of his character when the Prophet, at twenty, travelled with her goods, and he calls this one of the greatest choices. He says he will not now discuss the reasons for the other marriages. The Umar episode at 00:20:30 is garbled and is not written. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:25:00}
- He says some people tie istikhara to dreams and then back out, whereas its point is to hand the matter over to God and admit one owns nothing of it. He concludes that choice should not rest on looks, lineage or tribe, but on the other party's nearness to religion and sense of what is lawful and forbidden. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:22:00}
- He tells how Sa'id ibn al-Musayyib refused a proposal for his daughter from the son of the caliph and instead married her to his poor student Abdullah ibn Abi Wada'a, whose wife had died, knocking on his door at night and performing the marriage. The next morning, when Abdullah set off for Sa'id's lesson, his wife told him to sit and she would teach him Sa'id's knowledge, and she was a piece of it. He uses the story to show that religion is the measure of choice. This is the lecturer's telling, with no source named. (needs_source: the story is given without a source in the episode) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:24:00}
- He lists what a young man usually asks of a bride, beauty and height, and how a beautiful, wealthy wife may look down on her husband twenty or thirty years in. He then points to the Prophet's saying that a woman is married for four things, her wealth, beauty, lineage and religion, and to the instruction to win the one with religion. He says religion makes a woman content with little, patient, keeper of the household's secrets, covering faults and showing what is good. The text of the report is not typed here. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:28:30}
- Viewer calls follow. A mother in America says mothers of young men look for appearances and not religion. A caller from Abu Dhabi asks how to cope when one marries a person with psychological complexes, and what the signs of a good husband are. A woman asks whether 'the good for the good' is a divine command about spouses. A woman in Saudi Arabia who is engaged says people speak ill of her fiance. The calls are summarised from a garbled text, and some names and details are not written. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:32:00}
- More calls follow: a woman asks about marrying a man who is already married, without his first wife's knowledge (answered in episode two); a woman asks about a committed husband who changes over time; a man from Sudan proposed to three women and was refused after their istikhara; a woman asks the meaning of 'among your spouses and children are enemies to you' in [[passage/quran-64-14|verse 14 of Surat al-Taghabun]]; a Gulf caller asks how a suitor can know a girl from a respectable family; and a single mother in Sweden, with a son who has autism, complains of hard living and asks for a supplication. He postpones his answers to after the break. (needs_source: call details come from garbled text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:41:00}
- He answers: mothers and families should first look for the one with religion, which is not only hijab and memorising the Qur'an but general conduct. He thinks a psychological check should be added to the medical check for organic illnesses, and courses held on marital rights and duties (the lecturer's view). The way to know a person is to ask people of truthfulness and then make istikhara, and anyone asked as a witness about a suitor must tell the truth, since one who hides testimony is a sinner. On 'the good for the good' he argues it is not about spouses, because Nuh and Lut married women who did not believe, but about good words and deeds for the good, and he cites [[passage/quran-24-26|verse 26 of Surat al-Nur]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep01 00:44:00}

## Episode 2: choice between children and parents, and the rulings on marriage
- He opens by saying the first episode drew many reactions through the week, and that the subject of choice is sensitive for young and old. He announces a common fault he will address today: parents choosing their son's wife from their own sisters' and friends' daughters instead of the young man choosing. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:01:30}
- He cites children's complaint of a mother who says: if you do not marry your cousin I am angry with you until the Day of Resurrection. He says anger does not extend like that, and that a young man may see his cousin only as a sister. He holds that children's objection to parents' obstinacy is legitimate when marriage is delayed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:03:00}
- He sets out the five rulings of [[practice/nikah|marriage]]: obligatory for one who is certain he will fall into the forbidden if he does not marry, required for one who fears it, and recommended when both are equal, citing the warning against turning away from the Sunna. He says whoever obstructs marriage is a 'key to evil and a lock on good' and, in his own phrase, one who should see a psychiatrist, and he notes that unmarried women over the usual age number in the millions in some countries. These are his formulations and are not quoted from a particular school. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:06:30}
- He says marriage becomes disliked if one fears he will wrong his spouse, and forbidden if he is certain of it, giving examples of a withdrawn, ill-tempered young man or one lost in cafes, and a girl who cannot run a home. He says compatibility ([[concept/kafaa|kafa'a]]) exists in marriage but is not about tribe, country or family, and that the faults that matter are faults of behaviour. He points to the boasting of [[person/amr-ibn-kulthum|Amr ibn Kulthum]] about his tribe as a sign of tribal arrogance. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:09:30}
- He recommends a medical check before marriage and that neither party hides an illness from the other, since marriage is a solemn covenant and not an economic partnership. He suggests, as a matter of public interest (maslaha mursala), that the lawgiver also impose a psychological and behavioural examination on the suitor, with psychologists and sociologists working alongside jurists and doctors. This is the lecturer's own view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:12:00}
- On the parents' role: some choose a domineering husband for their daughter, though they would not want for her what they accept for other people's daughters. He holds that a generous man does not wrong, so the father should look for the groom's and his family's generosity, and he cites a saying attributed to an early figure that a daughter should be married to one who fears God, who will honour her if he loves her and not wrong her if he dislikes her. On a son's marriage the parents' opinion is advisory, and they should encourage his choice when compatibility, character and people's testimony are present. See [[concept/wali|the guardian]]. (needs_source: the saying is attributed to an early figure whose name is unclear in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:15:00}
- If the parents are not observant and the son has chosen a righteous girl, or if they approved of the girl at first and then magnified her faults after the engagement, he advises children not to press their parents except by persuasion, and advises parents that sons and daughters past twenty have their own standing and independence, so dialogue and consultation are needed. He adds that the Prophet consulted his wives and the Companions consulted theirs. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:19:30}
- He qualifies what he said in episode one after a discussion with scholars: the word 'imra'a' (woman or wife of) in the Qur'an can mark a difference in creation or conduct, or widowhood, or barrenness, such as the wife of [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]] and the barren wife of [[person/zakariyya|Zakariya]], who became his 'zawj' after God set her right for him. He says God grants females, or males, or both, or makes whom He wills barren, and that these four states are neither approval nor anger but wisdom, referring to [[passage/quran-42-49|verses 49]] and [[passage/quran-42-50|50 of Surat al-Shura]], and that children can be a trial, as in [[passage/quran-64-14|the verse of al-Taghabun]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:22:30}
- On marrying a widow or divorced woman: he objects to stopping the marriage because she was married before, saying the preference for virgins is a recommendation that does not lower a righteous previously married woman, and that a divorced couple may both be righteous yet not suited to each other. He holds that a son may propose a woman in whom he sees more good than fault. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:24:30}
- On the family's daughters: the father should not hurry and should ask about the suitor's commitment to the realities of religion, not merely his beard, and that three groups should speak well of him, his neighbours at home, his companions on a journey and those who deal with him in the markets (he specifies a journey of obedience). He says anyone asked about a suitor or a bride's father must say what he knows, not to block the marriage but to bear true witness, and notes that backbiting has six permitted cases that he will not go into now. See [[concept/ghayba|backbiting]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:27:30}
- He addresses his female viewers in school, university and past twenty not to be taken in by a modern civilisation that has made women a commodity on food packaging, nor by young men's good looks in films and serials. The most handsome young man, he says, is the one missed at the mosque because he is connected to God. Part of the window at 00:28:30 is a repetition loop. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:29:00}
- Calls follow: a husband complains that his wife left for her family's home after he asked her to stay at home; a woman whose husband divorced her in anger and then apologised; and a young woman asks whether her parents' prayer against her is a sign of disobedience on her part. The lecturer states plainly that the programme is not a fatwa programme but explains how safe homes are built on the method of the Qur'an and the Sunna. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:32:30}
- On the limits between suitor and fiancee: he is a stranger to her, so he does not see her hair, shake her hand or go out with her, and he reminds listeners of the Prophet's prohibition on proposing over a brother's proposal. To the caller who asked about marrying a man whose wife does not know, he says he is not comfortable with it and wishes her a husband whose situation is clear. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:36:00}
- On someone who was religious and then changed: he thinks the fault also lies with the other party, citing the Qur'anic statement that God set Zakariya's wife right for him: set right what is between you and God and God will set right what is between you and your wife. On the verse of [[passage/quran-64-14|al-Taghabun]] he says a spouse or child can be a trial when a father is driven to doubtful or forbidden earnings to please his household, recalls women Companions who held their husbands' hands as they left for work and said they could bear hunger but not the fire of Hell, and says forbidden money wipes out blessing and that rebellious children and weary spouses may come from forbidden money in the home. The remark about the women Companions is the lecturer's telling, with no name given. (needs_source: the story of the women Companions has no name or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:38:30}
- He corrects a caller that Gulf society is a society of values, not a closed one, and that getting to know one another is through visits at the girl's family home and in the family's presence, not telephone calls until just before dawn after which the couple fall silent after marriage, and he urges 'rationing of consumption and spending even in speech'. He says other traits will appear after the wedding however long the engagement, and that one who stays within God's limits is at ease. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:40:30}
- On the late condition that a wife stay at home: he says this condition should have been agreed before the contract, and that after the contract a daughter is in the care of another man, who is the person responsible for her, and he reports a saying of the Prophet that the one with the most right over a man is his mother and over a woman is her husband (the wording is not typed). He criticises using the telephone for a mother to run her daughter's home from afar. See [[concept/huquq-al-zawjayn|rights of the spouses]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:42:30}
- On parents' prayers against their children he warns of them, and where the newly married couple clash with the girl's family he thinks it better for the couple to live apart in a new home, since houses cannot hold everyone, and a daughter's life may not go well with her mother either. The last part of this passage is garbled. (needs_source: the end of the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep02 00:43:30}

## Episode 3: the engagement and its manners
- He begins with the engagement (khitba) and calls it an old Pharaonic and Greek custom. He says that among the Greeks the groom rode a horse and tied his bride with an iron fetter so that she ran behind him, and that the English invented the engagement ring, worn on one finger and moved to the other at the wedding. This is the lecturer's claim and no source is given. See [[concept/khitba-fiqh|the engagement]]. (needs_source: the origin of engagement and the ring is the lecturer's claim without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:02:30}
- He criticises a wife who makes a storm if her husband takes off the ring or it becomes tight, saying that whoever cares about trivial things demolishes his home. He sees no harm, in his view, in wearing a ring with a particular intention, but warns against a man wearing a gold ring, since gold is forbidden to men and lawful for women, and thinks the real ring should be a firm covenant to uphold God's law in the home. The ruling is stated as the lecturer's own. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:05:00}
- He says God created male and female from one source but with different tasks, so they are complementary kinds, not opposites, and that some writing demeans women. He compares a woman in the West flying a warplane with a Muslim woman barred from driving a car, and calls for a realistic reading from the Qur'an and the Sunna, neither lax nor narrow, saying that charging women with a deficiency of reason leads some men to exercise guardianship through a wrong understanding. These are the lecturer's views on a disputed matter. See [[concept/al-qiwama|guardianship (qiwama)]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:09:00}
- He says homes teach the boy from childhood that service belongs to mother and sister, so he does not wash his own plate and later looks down on his fiancee, and he sees this as coming from the Greek idea of dragging women along and not from religion. He criticises the court-ordered 'house of obedience' and says such places are not safe homes but graves. He calls for a new way of looking at the other party. This is his own way of putting his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:11:30}
- He explains that guardianship rests with the man because he proposes, contracts, pays the [[concept/mahr|dower]] and spends, so it is a charge before it is an honour, and if a man withdraws from the charge there is no honour. It is not money alone, nor a reaching out of the hand or tongue to wound, but care. He warns of what comes from a busy father, a neglectful mother and doubtful money. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:14:30}
- He distinguishes a 'static' engagement inside the home from a 'dynamic' one. He then says the Prophet ordered that the engagement be kept quiet (as the lecturer reports it), and lists the wisdom: people spy on strange cars, neighbours question a small child who will not lie because his father taught him not to, and the bride's family may tell relatives, making it a burden. If the match does not work, both sides withdraw quietly, without hurt or the question 'why did your first fiance leave you?'. Prevention is better than cure. The attribution of the order to the Prophet is the lecturer's, and it is not verified here. (needs_source: the order to keep the engagement secret is attributed to the Prophet with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:19:00}
- In the static engagement the suitor comes polished and says well-chosen words, whereas in the dynamic one, with the whole family going out, his character, generosity and temper show. He stresses that the suitor remains a stranger to the fiancee, seeing only her face and hands, with the outing being with the whole family, and that a long engagement plants problems. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:21:30}
- On istikhara he says both suitor and fiancee make it, and that the father and mother may too, and summarises the supplication. He criticises a girl who told him she had made istikhara when she does not pray, wear hijab or read the Qur'an, and says istikhara must be done free of desire. He says religion and character are the measure: if someone comes whose religion and character you approve, marry him, or else there will be trial and great corruption (reported in sense by the lecturer). He tells his female viewers that beauty is in faith, hijab and modesty, that heavy make-up attracts flies, and recalls the Qur'an's description of the women of Paradise as 'restraining their glances' ([[passage/quran-55-56|al-Rahman 56]]). See [[practice/istikhara|istikhara]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:26:00}
- He advises girls not to follow bad company and to ask the mosque imam about a good family, and says parents who do not encourage their daughter to wear hijab fail their children, especially if they leave the television on songs and films all day. He repeats that keeping the engagement quiet protects the home's secrets. He postpones the question about the couple's telephone calls to after the break. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:28:00}
- Among the calls: a husband who agreed with his wife to name their child after her brother, then the two brothers fell out. The lecturer thinks that if the name is good and not odd there is no objection, that decisions in permitted matters should not be dictatorial, and that a boat has two rowers but one decision so that it can dock. A caller raises the problem of children torn apart after divorce, and he says this is the subject of upcoming episodes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:32:00}
- A caller asks about wearing a wig and a statement attributed to al-Shafi'i, and the lecturer replies that jurisprudential questions should go to the fiqh programme run by his colleague, and that he is speaking only about the engagement. A man asks about his fifteen-year-old daughter who loves singing to excess, and the answer is postponed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:35:00}
- He says istikhara may end in a trial for one of the two parties, a test lasting years. He warns against haste in divorce and says that if divorce is needed it must not be at the children's expense, that neither parent should blacken the other before the child, and that spouses should be 'great' in their divorce as in their marriage, since wronging the other brings a heavy consequence on the Day of Resurrection. (needs_source: part of the discussion is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:39:30}
- He faults the suitor who holds thousands of conversations with his fiancee and then falls mute and deaf after the wedding. He cites a recent statistic, as he puts it, that an Arab man speaks about 8,000 words a day and an Arab woman about 17,000, and that after marriage none of them is for the spouse, which he counts as an effect of leaving the legal frame. (needs_source: the statistic has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:41:30}
- He answers other questions: to someone whose friend is drawn to immodest dress and make-up, he says embrace her as a friend, then draw near and give gentle, indirect advice. To the one with the daughter who loves singing, he says increase her Qur'an reading little by little and that parents should beware spending their own time on songs. On the prohibition of proposing over a brother's proposal, he says the one who has announced the engagement should tell anyone who wants to propose that she is engaged, notes that engagement parties can cost thousands, and postpones the dower, the gift of jewellery and the household outfit to later episodes. To a husband who taunts his wife for having become observant after a less committed past, he says we all have faults, so treat her in a way that pleases God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep03 00:46:00}

## Episode 4: the legal viewing and frankness between the two parties
- He reminds viewers that an engagement is a promise of marriage that creates no rights, and says the keeping of engagements quiet, which he discussed last week, is hard for people in his time who like show and spreading secrets and ask about strange cars outside a house. He holds that a Muslim asks only about what benefits him. See [[concept/khitba-fiqh|the engagement]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:03:00}
- He sees the engagement today as caught between excess and neglect: Arab families where the suitor does not see his fiancee until the contract, and others where he goes out with her, travels with her and dines with her in cafes. He cites the Prophet telling Jabir to look at the woman he was proposing to, and says Jabir used to hide under a tree to see something that would encourage him to marry her (reported in sense). He says the law allows seeing her face and hands in her family's presence, that the question of hair is outside the episode, and that this is a middle community with neither excess nor neglect. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:06:30}
- He requires frankness between the two parties about illnesses and defects: a woman with Mediterranean fever should tell her suitor and tell him her medicines and symptoms, and a husband who finds after the wedding that his wife hid an illness that prevents pregnancy has a problem. He notes that some countries, such as the Emirates and Egypt, require a medical certificate. He says infertility is by God's decree ([[passage/quran-42-49|al-Shura 49]]), but the other party must be told from the start, because whoever cheats is not one of us (reported in sense). See [[passage/quran-42-50|verse 50]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:09:30}
- He adds that homes rest on reality and not on affection alone, so the suitor should state his debts and the bride's family their problems. He repeats the difference between the quiet and the moving engagement: the suitor who sits politely and holds his cup with manners may turn out after the wedding to be foul-tongued or bad-tempered, and polished speech does not build a home, so both sides should be plain and unaffected. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:12:30}
- He advises a family who have a first suitor and then a better one not to keep the first waiting: if no word has been given, close the door on the first before the second comes, because daughters are not goods sold to the highest bidder. One does not compare two salaries or two positions; one makes istikhara and accepts or declines. He says this is the clarity expected of a Muslim. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:14:30}
- On the suitor's religion: the family should ask about his prayer, for one who does not keep his covenant with God cannot be trusted to keep faith with their daughter. He says prayer is a sign, not the only measure, since a man may pray and still cheat, but it shows he keeps his covenant with God. He notes that the girl may ask the suitor how much Qur'an he has memorised and about his prayer. He cites, in sense, the Prophet's saying that if one comes whose religion and character you approve you should marry him, or there will be trial and great corruption. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:16:00}
- On the bride's family receiving the suitor: the wise young man looks for a good mother, since a good mother raises a good daughter, and also asks about the father, saying what is wanted is a father with the quality of manliness (rujula), not just maleness, and that the Qur'an describes the qualities of such men. He tells the suitor not to treat the visit as an exam of fifty true-or-false questions but to behave naturally. He says a girl respects the man who respects his manliness in dress, character and speech, neither coarse nor soft like an effeminate man. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:19:30}
- He warns against dragging out an engagement because faults begin to show, and against contracting the marriage while the bride stays at her father's house ('marriage with execution suspended'), because the question arises of who leads the girl, her father or her husband. He acknowledges that economic difficulty today pushes families to contract so that going out together is lawful. He advises the young man who has contracted to ask permission of the bride's father, honour him and give him his due, so that he overlooks his faults and treats him as a son. These are the lecturer's views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:23:30}
- On the shabka (the engagement jewellery): it is not a pillar of marriage but people are used to it, and in some countries it counts as part of the dower and takes its ruling, while in others it is a gift and the giver may take it back. He criticises a bride's family who tell the suitor to buy what he can and then add that her sister's fiance brought fifty thousand. He calls for making the lawful easy so the forbidden becomes hard, and for not being fooled by show. See [[practice/mahr|the dower]] and [[concept/hiba-fiqh|gifts]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:27:00}
- He asks parents not to relay every small and large matter to the two sides after the contract, since the families grow used to spreading news. He recounts the counsel of the wife of Awf ibn Muhallim al-Shaybani to her daughter on her wedding: you have left the nest you grew up in for a nest you do not know and a companion you are not used to, so be like earth to him and he will be like sky to you (given in sense). He pictures the groom's family as A, the bride's as B and the new family as C, each wanting C to orbit around it, and says: leave the new family to be built on the Qur'an, the Sunna, affection and mercy. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:29:30}
- He says a family is not mended by treating the world with the world, and the real remedy is that each person set right what is between himself and his Lord so that God sets right what is between him and the other party, citing God's setting right of [[person/zakariyya|Zakariya]]'s wife ([[passage/quran-21-90|al-Anbiya 90]]). He again stresses that homes stand on grace and not bare justice, and promises to explain the difference. See [[concept/adl-wal-fadl|justice and grace]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:31:00}
- In the calls: a caller from Tunisia whose fiance broke the engagement without reason and who sold the engagement ring asks whether she must repay its price; a caller from Syria, a second wife by contract, asks how to ask for security; a caller asks about a subject outside the episode, and a mother asks about books for her children and plastic Qur'an copies; a caller from Algeria whose suitor comes tomorrow asks whether she may be alone with him at the viewing. The call details come from garbled text. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:34:00}
- He answers: the engagement jewellery and presents are gifts, and the giver may take them back, but decency and kindness call for the broken-off suitor to leave what was sold of the ring, and he postpones the detailed rulings to later episodes. A debt owed to someone who died goes to his heirs. A contract binds the parties so long as it does not make the forbidden lawful or the lawful forbidden, so she may ask for what secures her if he can afford it, and dealings should be by grace and not strict justice, 'and do not forget grace between you'. The legal viewing is with the family present, not alone, in which the face and hands are seen and one hears how she speaks with her family. To a woman who sees childlessness as a trial: God grants females and males and makes whom He wills barren, and that is not a trial (verses cited in episode two). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:42:00}
- He says that homes built on justice alone leave each party claiming rights and forgetting duties, whereas on grace a person closes his eyes to some of the other's faults. He recounts of the Prophet's household that he told [[person/aisha|Aisha]] he knew when she was pleased with him and when angry: when pleased she swore by the Lord of Muhammad, and when angry by the Lord of Ibrahim, to which she replied that she only stays away with her tongue and he remains in her heart. He infers that the Prophet had a large credit with her and so overlooked her anger. He tells of [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] being invited by a man to mutual reproach and answering 'let us forgive one another', and advises hearing an angry person out and leaving him until he calms. This is the lecturer's retelling. (needs_source: the accounts of the Prophet and Aisha and of al-Hasan al-Basri are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:44:00}
- He advises engaged couples not to broadcast their households' secrets to the other: the suitor should not say his sister is rebellious, his father proud or his mother foolish, nor the fiancee likewise, since people recall the bad and forget the good. He says one of the greatest betrayals before God is for a man to confide in his wife and she tell her friends, a report the lecturer gives in sense with no chain. After the contract the girl remains with her father, so asking permission and good manners are observed. (needs_source: the report on the greatest betrayals has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep04 00:45:30}

## Episode 5: 'that you may find tranquillity in them' — the start of married life
- He opens with a prayer that Muslims' homes be safe and at rest, then explains that the 'dwelling' in the verse is not four walls, for many spacious houses are like graves with no tranquillity, affection or mercy, and many narrow ones have room in every heart. See [[passage/quran-30-21|verse 21 of Surat al-Rum]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:02:00}
- He turns to the start of married life after the contract and wedding and frames it with a fence of faith. He relays a news-agency item, as he tells it, about a non-Muslim man whose wife was in a coma for ten months and whose doctors had lost hope; he sat by her speaking kind words, she woke, and scholars who studied the case found that 98 or 99 per cent of her recovery was due to the kind words. He concludes that charging a fiancee against her fiance and his family from the start produces 'the wreckage of a girl'. (needs_source: the coma item and the percentage have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:05:30}
- He criticises racism among Arabs: when a man from the north of a country marries a woman from the south, partisanship flares, and Arabs without religion, he says, are racist of the first degree, citing a saying of [[person/ibn-khaldun|Ibn Khaldun]] in the Muqaddima, in sense, that the Arab does not do well without religion. He says our duty is to set right our children's souls before the marital nest and to give them positive counsels, not negative ones. The attribution to Ibn Khaldun is the lecturer's. (needs_source: the saying attributed to Ibn Khaldun has no text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:07:30}
- He tells the young husband not to disparage his sister-in-law or his wife, and to look for her merits and not her shortcomings, noting that when one finger points at a fault three fingers point back at the pointer. He says spouses should not count each other's mistakes but live in the space of the good. These are the lecturer's counsels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:10:30}
- On the first night: he says the first thing the couple do is pray two rak'as, and the husband puts his hand on his wife's forelock and asks God for her good and the good in her and seeks refuge from her harm, which he says is the Sunna of the Prophet, and that a wedding night of noise and disorder instead is not a safe home. The detailed form of the prayer is the lecturer's and its source is not verified here. (needs_source: the form of the wedding-night prayer has no source in the episode) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:12:00}
- He calls for respecting each country's and tribe's customs so long as they do not clash with the Qur'an and the Sunna, notes that [[person/ibn-hazm|Ibn Hazm]] sees compatibility ([[concept/kafaa|kafa'a]]) in religion alone and [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]] in religion and lineage, and leans toward Ahmad's view. He then digresses to criticise the community as consumers and not producers: the camera, watch, pen and paper are made by others, so pride belongs only to religion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:15:00}
- He states that the injustice that falls on the community from the world will not lift until we lift injustice from our homes, and that the wronged person prays in the dead of night. He recalls the sacred report that God forbade wrong to Himself and made it forbidden among His servants (given in sense), and that wrong is darknesses on the Day of Resurrection, where retribution is by good and bad deeds and not coins. He holds the worst wrong is wrong to those one supports, and that some husbands are tied to their wives by nothing but a paper. See [[concept/zulm|injustice]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:18:00}
- He says that in an Arab Muslim country there is a divorce every six minutes, and that in the United States a husband assaults his wife every twenty-seven seconds, 49 per cent of it psychological, and asks where the reformers, imams, programmes and intensive courses to prepare young people for marriage are. These figures are given by the lecturer with no source or date. (needs_source: the figures have no source or date) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:20:00}
- He holds that the best remedy for crises is supplication, and that dignity belongs to the believers, not through exalting themselves over one another, citing the Qur'an on Badr ([[passage/quran-3-123|Al Imran 123]]) that God gave them victory when they were weak. He then suggests the couple agree from the first day that their reference is the Qur'an and the Sunna, measuring deeds against them as lengths are measured by the metre and earthquakes by the Richter scale. He pictures a home with songs and music playing all day and says [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]], [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] would not enter it if invited, and that questions arise only where there is doubt. This is the lecturer's view on a disputed matter. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:23:30}
- He advises the wife to ask her husband what he likes and dislikes and to avoid discussing things with him in a moment of anger, recalling that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], on becoming caliph, said in effect that he had a devil that overcame him and that if they saw him angry they should avoid him (a saying the lecturer gives with no source). He advises the husband not to neglect his wife or treat her as a discarded item, but to say 'a little later' so that there is a language of dialogue. He promises to treat the difference between grace and justice after the break. (needs_source: the saying of Abu Bakr has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:26:30}
- Calls: a woman who was divorced and returned to her husband, who spends much time out of the house, thinks of divorce and has no children, and he answers that a person bears so long as the good outweighs the bad, and that if endurance falls to zero then yes, otherwise there is no need for divorce; a 45-year-old caller from Syria whose father rejects every suitor, and he appeals to the father not to obstruct her; a man who signed his contract and uttered a prohibiting oath over his wife before consummation, asking its ruling; and a wife who asks about her husband postponing children after a miscarriage, to whom he says God grants females and males and makes whom He wills barren. The call details come from garbled text, and the answer on the prohibiting oath is not clear in the recording. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep05 00:32:30}

## Episode 6: the pillars of the home and the winds of affection
- He reviews what came before: the difference between engagement and contract, the couple's move to the marital nest, and the pillars on which God set the home, three: tranquillity, affection and mercy. He says God put three steps before separation: if tranquillity is lost affection and mercy remain, if affection is lost mercy remains, and if all are lost nothing of the home is left. This ordering is the lecturer's own reading. See [[passage/quran-30-21|verse 21 of Surat al-Rum]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:02:30}
- He says the politeness of the quiet engagement does not reveal temperaments, so wife and husband discover after the move things that did not show in the visits. He states that marriage in Islam is a worldly and an other-worldly project, not only an economic or social one, and that the relation between spouses is obedience to God. He recalls a prayer for mercy on a husband who wakes his wife for prayer and sprinkles water on her face, and on a wife who does so for her husband (given in sense, a report whose source he does not name). (needs_source: the report on waking one another for prayer has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:05:00}
- He complains that grievances arrive in the first month, which the West calls the honeymoon, and that a wife may go back to her family's house because her husband snores or a cup of tea was late. He advises the couple not to judge the other's temperament before at least a year, since some people live twenty and thirty years together and one finds in the other what he never knew, and that a wife should not compare her husband with her sisters' husbands. He sorts people into quick to anger and quick to be pleased, slow to anger and slow to be pleased, and quick to anger and slow to be pleased, the worst of them, who needs patience and training. He says a husband may show admiration with a smile that is the most he can offer while a woman wants detailed words, and advises asking the husband's mother what he likes and what angers him. This is the lecturer's classification and opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:08:30}
- He says one pillar of the home is tolerating slips, and that civilisational progress does not happen in wartime, nor do homes bear fruit under psychological attack. He warns of two keys that demolish a home: jealousy out of place, 'the key to divorce', and too much reproach, 'the key to hatred'. He says Arabs do not excel at the manners of apology, and that repeated reproach over small things demolishes. He gives the example of one who dwells on five faults and forgets seventy good deeds, and of one who is angry because his wife gained two kilograms. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:13:00}
- He advises the newly married wife not to listen to women who relay their bitter experiences, nor to friends who are not observant, and advises the husband not to listen to friends who keep him from his wife. He says obeying one's husband in what is right opens the home, and urges the wife not to look down on him because of her money, family or beauty, citing a line of verse on a beautiful face with an ugly soul. He says a home is built on three things, sacrifice, loyalty and virtue, that guardianship is not muscle but intelligence and good conduct, and that dividing roles prevents frustration. These are the lecturer's counsels. See [[concept/al-qiwama|guardianship]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:17:30}
- He explains why he named the episode 'winds of affection' in the plural: winds bring mercy and give good news of it, whereas a single wind may be a gale. He says affection is wider and more inclusive than love, and tells of a man in the time of [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] who wanted to divorce his wife because he did not love her, and Umar struck him with his whip and said: where is care and consideration? (the lecturer gives it with no source). He speaks of mercy as familiarity, and says love covers faults while dislike shows flaws. (needs_source: the story of the man and Umar has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:20:00}
- Calls: a woman from Abu Dhabi asks for a prayer for those not yet given children and about a friend who learned her husband knows another woman; a caller from Iraq complains of problems with her husband; a man repeats his question about a prohibiting oath before consummation; a woman asks about two nursings of her husband's sister's son and about marriage to him; and a woman says her husband ties divorce to everything and fears she is forbidden to him. The lecturer refers the legal questions to fatwa programmes. The call details come from garbled text. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:24:30}
- He answers: he prays for children for those without, and tells the wife who learned her husband knows another woman not to treat him as a dull pupil before a headmistress with a cane, but to look at the lack that drove him and win him with prayer and good treatment. He says the way to a happy married life is obeying God, looking at faults and not magnifying them, and that lawful food brings tranquillity, affection and mercy while forbidden food brings ruin (he alludes to texts without naming them). On suckling he thinks keeping away from doubt is better, noting the disagreement over the number of nursings, and on suspended divorce he advises the husband to go to a jurist himself, saying fatwas on air are risky because each fatwa has its setting. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:30:00}
- More calls: a woman married twenty-seven years who found her husband had secretly contracted marriage to another woman and then cancelled it and returned, and cannot forget it; a newly married man asks about his wife's menstruation; a caller asks about the zakat on a shop bought on instalments; and a woman asks about a lost item found in the street. He answers the first that she should pardon and overlook, that reproach is the door to hatred, and that she should treat it as though it did not happen, because a husband who finds no rest at home looks for it elsewhere, and he cites the Qur'an on pardon and reconciliation ([[passage/quran-42-40|al-Shura 40]]). He refers the other questions to the phone after the programme. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:35:30}
- He sums up the episode as counsels to the new wife: set right what is between her and God, seek no pretexts, not listen to the advice of a former circle that is not observant, show her husband care and esteem, forgive him when he errs, clear his family as far as she can unless they truly harm her, call him by the name he likes best, and receive him with a smile, a good appearance and, if possible, a pleasant scent. These are the lecturer's counsels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep06 00:38:00}

## Episode 7: the first year of marriage
- He announces the subject of the episode: the first year of marriage, the hardest in planting either the tree of affection or the seed of discord: the couple sow either a seed of affection that grows into love and good, or a seed of discord and fixing on the other's faults. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:02:00}
- He gives two realities. First, that in the Arab world there is 'an abundance of maleness and a scarcity of manliness', and that God gave guardianship to the man because his reasoning comes before his emotion. Second, that many complain that some women do not give what their mothers and grandmothers gave of affection, tranquillity and patience, and that some look down on others through money, job or family while the spirit of religion is absent, citing an Arabic proverb on roles thrown into disorder. See [[concept/al-qiwama|guardianship]]. This is the lecturer's opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:03:30}
- He gives the example of two sisters who married two brothers on the same day: the first complains that her husband is cold and calm, the second that he is tense, and each wishes for the other's husband's nature. He says he wants neither a deadly calm nor a blazing fire but a man with the quality of manliness who likes someone to share and discuss with him, and that a wife may be passive and take no decision. He puts a formula of 'democratic in talking, dictatorial in deciding', then corrects himself that he does not want that but familiarity and complementarity. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:06:30}
- He advises the husband and wife in the first year to listen and to accept differences in temperament and in everyday habits (sleep, waking, food) where nothing lawful or forbidden is at stake, not to straighten the other's bend all at once, as a mental patient is not treated in a single session, and to weigh their words as a goldsmith weighs gold, since wounds of the tongue cut deeper than wounds of the spear. He says the Arabs used to praise a girl as 'sleeping till mid-morning', meaning she had servants, and says things are different now. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep07 00:10:30}
- He says Arabs do not accept criticism but do accept sincere, wrapped advice, and that the husband is not a child nor the wife a school headmistress or 'ruler by your own command'. He gives the example of [[person/al-hakim-bi-amr-allah|al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah]], who banned various things for people in Egypt, and counts that as the jurisprudence of maleness and not of manliness.
