He warns a mother who taunts her son for his weight so he is discouraged and grows a complex. This is the lecturer's example. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:18:30}
- He says we should complain of ourselves that we have not embraced our children, and cites a statistic that the American father stays with his son three minutes a day and the Arab three minutes a year (a claim without source). He criticises a mother who looks at one weak subject and ignores seven excellent ones, and tells that the Prophet, when brought the first fruit, would give it to the youngest child and would stroke children's heads on the road (given in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the minutes statistic and the report of the Prophet and the fruit have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:20:30}
- He calls on father and mother to forget the children's slights, for none of us is perfect, citing the saying 'the dullard is not the master of his people, rather their master is the one who feigns dullness', and says if people did not sin God would take them away and bring a people who sin and ask forgiveness (stated in sense). He says children carry pressures, so we should be a source of protection and not of pressure, and mentions the 'orphan' whose father is present yet absent. He thanks teachers who bear hundreds of pupils. These are the lecturer's counsels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:35:00}
- Calls: a woman says her son, between forty and fifty, has become short-tempered with his mother and brothers and refuses to pay for the household; a caller from Iraq wants to move with his wife to America; a woman whose husband admitted a relationship with a foreigner and regrets it; a mother of three, sharp in temper, asks how to control herself; a caller comments on the Friday preacher and those who leave prayer; and a caller asks about the report 'if I were to order anyone to prostrate to other than God'. The call details come from garbled text. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:42:30}
- In the answers: to the angry father he describes the other party absorbing shocks like the rubber tyres on quaysides, perhaps because of pressures on him; to the Iraqi that stability in a Muslim country where the call to prayer is heard is good; to the one whose husband repented he prays she accept him, for we all have faults and God pardons those who pardon; to the sharp-tempered mother he recalls that Umar prayed that God soften his heart and place his harshness on the people of falsehood (a saying without a chain); to a young man estranged from his father he advises sending elders to mend; to one who objects to the Friday preacher he says we strengthen believing immunity in children and cannot kill every microbe; and to a divorced woman still in the house she observes a waiting period of three courses. He closes with a prayer for our homes. This is the lecturer's handling of the answers. (needs_source: the saying of Umar on harshness has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep23 00:52:00}

## Episode 20 (continued): the calls and the answers
- Calls: a caller prays for the security of Egypt and the Arab and Islamic world and asks the lecturer for his prayers, then another complains that his married brother always puts his wife first, so their mother is sad, and that he cannot speak with his brother. Part of the text is cut off. (needs_source: the end of the call is cut off) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:37:30}
- More calls: a woman asks how to deal with people who hold a hard line, and a Syrian resident in Saudi Arabia raises a question on which there is much disagreement. The text of both questions and the lecturer's reply is garbled and falls into repetition, so nothing of them is recorded. (needs_source: the text of the two questions and the answer is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:39:00}
- In the answer he presents the order of priorities as 'emptying before adorning': that a person first erases bad traits such as rancour, envy, backbiting and looking for people's faults, then adorns himself with good works and spreading security in society. A caller adds dignity along with chivalry, and the lecturer replies that planting it and truthfulness in children comes by our being the example, for none of us can plant chivalry while turning down everyone who asks him. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:40:30}
- On the proud who amass wealth, he prays that God show them their faults in the mirror of the heart so that they see their pride, and says that gathering money is not evil in itself, the evil being to gather it from what is forbidden or spend it where God has not permitted. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:43:00}
- A caller from Jordan says he married a woman who did not wear the veil, that she objected when he asked her to wear it at a wedding and a big problem arose, and asks the lecturer's opinion. The lecturer's reply on hijab comes later in the episode. See [[concept/hijab|hijab]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:41:30}
- A caller from Italy reproaches his married brother for listening to his wife and not to his mother. The lecturer says this is an instance of what is reported, that among the signs of the Hour a man obeys his wife and neglects his mother (stated in sense), then notes that some young men set their mother in one pan of the scale and their wife in the other, yet they are not to be set in two pans: the mother has one kind of rights and the wife another, and neither right is to encroach on the other, comparing the confusion to adding papers to morals. He prays that God watch over the mother's heart, and that the son carry to his wife nothing of what his mother does nor to his mother anything of what his wife does. See [[practice/birr-al-walidayn|honouring parents]]. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the report on the signs of the Hour is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:45:00}
- To a caller living in the Kingdom who asks about the niqab, the lecturer says that a woman who wears it as an obligation must not take it off, while one who wears it as a virtue is in a matter of disagreement among jurists, an accepted disagreement, which is not made a binding ruling, and he thanks women who wear it. See [[concept/niqab|niqab]]. This is the lecturer's view on a disputed question. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:45:30}
- To the Jordanian caller whose wife does not wear the veil, he says he fears a hard reckoning for him on the Day of Resurrection, that everyone who looks at his wife unveiled takes a burden which is placed in the scale of his bad deeds (a claim without source), and hopes his wife is listening, saying this is a command from God and not from the husband. This is the lecturer's view on a disputed question. (needs_source: the claim of a burden for every onlooker has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:46:00}
- Umm Mahmoud asks, after her mother's death, whether the mother is harmed when she weeps each time she remembers her. The lecturer answers that, God willing, there is no harm, that weeping is mercy, compassion and tenderness from a daughter's heart for the mother who raised her, and that she should honour her by praying for her in every prayer. He ends by saying Muslims' personal problems are what concern them most in the questions, and prays that God show them the defect and mend what is between them. This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep20 00:47:00}

## Episode 21 (continued): the calls and the answers
- Calls: a caller asks prayer for lawful provision, and a caller from Dubai asks how to advise an older person to avoid falling into sins and how to teach another to advise him, and the lecturer refers callers to the studio numbers. The text is cut off. (needs_source: the text of the question is cut off) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:37:30}
- A caller asks about the person who goes on pilgrimage every year while his neighbour is poor. The lecturer answers that those who perform the pilgrimage every year while they have poor neighbours and children in Somalia are in a grave matter, and says that if half a million of those who had already performed it put that money there Somalia would become richer than America (an exaggeration with no source), and that bringing joy to Muslims is an obligation while the voluntary act comes after the obligation. See [[practice/hajj|hajj]]. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the pilgrim number and the claimed effect of the money in Somalia have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:43:00}
- A caller from Algeria connects and is asked to turn down his television, after which the studio number is repeated for viewers without his question being heard. There is no substantive material in this passage. (needs_source: the caller's question is not heard in the text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:39:00}
- Samia from Dubai asks about the limits of dealing with Christian relatives at feasts, occasions and mixing. The lecturer answers, of her sister's children who are Christian, that what is asked is conduct of mercy, and recalls that Asma asked about keeping ties with her mother and was told to keep ties with her mother (given in sense), and that it is a Muslim's duty to keep these family ties so that Islam becomes clear to them. See [[person/asma-bint-abi-bakr|Asma bint Abi Bakr]]. This is the lecturer's answer. (needs_source: the report of Asma's question about her mother is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:43:30}
- A woman from Jordan praises the programme and asks the lecturer to pray for righteous offspring for her, and he prays for her. There is no substantive material in this passage. (needs_source: there is no substantive material in the passage) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:40:00}
- A caller asks about the owner of a building who employs people in it where indecent acts occur without his knowing, whether he bears anything. The lecturer replies that the owner of the building is not asked to inspect the place where people live, since that is not normal. This is the lecturer's answer, and the opening of the reply is garbled. (needs_source: the opening of the reply is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:44:00}
- The lecturer says sins are major and minor, the major being that on which a text has come and the minor that on which none has, and states in sense that there is no minor with persistence and no major with seeking forgiveness. He calls for upbringing to begin by teaching children the lawful and forbidden from the start and for scholars not to fall short in reminding, since a person always needs one to remind him. On advising, he says the nature of the one advised differs: one who asks we lend our ear, another we remind of God's favours to him, his good deeds and works, then show him that this does not mean he should fall into sin. This is the lecturer's classification. (needs_source: the saying 'no minor with persistence' is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:42:00}
- In closing he says a society cannot do without knowledge that points to the hereafter, or a companion who helps toward obedience and keeps from disobedience, or knowing the enemy who is Satan, and having a lesson to learn from (the end of the sentence is garbled), and prays that God secure the lands of Muslims and spare their blood. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the end of the sentence is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep21 00:44:30}

## Episode 22 (continued): the calls and the answers
- Ahlam says her husband respects her but she is very sensitive, so that when he speaks before a group she feels he is mocking her, and that he is trying to understand and take care. The lecturer answers her to lighten her sensitivity and take the words as joking, and that the husband, since he knows this, should not press on the wound at the moment of pain. This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:44:00}
- A woman says her husband is short-tempered, a smoker, and accepts advice from no one, and that whenever she reminds him of his prayer and his Lord he grows angry. The lecturer answers that there is no need for constant reminding with shouting, then adds that the text is weighty so one should not turn it into an argument (the rest of the phrase is garbled). This is the lecturer's answer. (needs_source: the rest of the phrase is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:44:30}
- A woman describes her husband as devout and wonderful but changing suddenly in anger, saying unkind words and sometimes going beyond limits, and says she does not know the key to his personality. The lecturer responds that she wants to look for the key to his personality. Personal details in the text are garbled and are not recorded. See the same episode on the keys to the personality. (needs_source: the details of the call are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:34:00}
- Umm Ahmad says her husband borrows from banks and from his friends to give money to his family, and that she is against a bank loan; the lecturer asks her whether his family are needy to that degree. The conversation is then moved to the telephone off the air. No answer is recorded in the passage. See [[concept/riba|usury]]. (needs_source: no answer is recorded and no clear text gives a ruling on the loan) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:35:30}
- Abdullah thanks the lecturer and asks for books or sites for further study, then his wife says her husband is patient but she is sensitive, that at the weekend he is with guests or family and laughs and is lively whereas on workdays he comes home tired and does not want to speak, that she feels psychologically worn and weeps, and asks for the key to her husband's personality. No answer is recorded in the passage, and the line is cut at its end. (needs_source: no answer is recorded and the line is cut) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:37:30}
- Amira asks about her husband who works in a bank, whether what is spent on them is lawful or forbidden, and says they have tried with him about prayer and he responds only at times. The text of the reply is garbled, so no ruling or answer is recorded. (needs_source: the text of the reply is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:39:00}
- A woman from Saudi Arabia asks for the lecturer's number and the passage falls into repetition, so nothing of it is recorded. (needs_source: the passage is only repetition) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:39:30}
- Of a woman married four years who became pregnant after great effort and has problems with her husband's mother, the lecturer says a person should look at the favours, that God gave her a pregnancy after a long while, that differing viewpoints with the husband's mother are a kind of trial, that she should bear in mind she is his mother, and that some husbands confuse wives' rights and mothers' rights, an issue needing balance which he will expand on in coming episodes. He calls for patience. This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:41:30}
- Umm Ru'ya says she is at the start of married life, that her husband says she does not respect him and that when she is angry she goes beyond limits. The lecturer says that when a person is irritable by nature, there is no need at the moment of anger to offer opinions or ask questions, and tells that Abu Bakr used to say 'I have a devil that comes over me, so if you see me angry keep away from me' (given in sense with no chain), and advises one who gets angry to seek refuge in God from the accursed Satan and shut himself in a room. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]. This is the lecturer's answer, and whom the reply addresses is garbled. (needs_source: the saying of Abu Bakr is in sense without a chain and whom the reply addresses is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:42:30}
- On a husband's pride, he says the wife should welcome him at the door and see him off each day to the door with a prayer for him, which leaves a store of goodwill, and says hearts are disposed to love those who do good to them and to dislike those who do wrong (stated in sense with no chain). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the saying on loving one who does good is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:43:30}
- He says the time has passed quickly and the remaining questions are deferred to the next episode, answers briefly a woman whose husband fears God and has performed the lesser pilgrimage but fluctuates that patience with him opens relief, and promises to explain the keys to the personality further next week. This is the lecturer's announcement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep22 00:45:00}

## Episode 24: truthfulness and lying in the home
- He opens with the virtuous city: one in which only the voice of truth is raised, each resident knowing his right and not taking more than it, and his duty and fulfilling it. He says it existed in the age of the rightly guided caliphs, and tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] held the judgeship under [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] for a full year and resigned because not one litigant came to him (his telling, without a chain). He attributes to [[person/al-farabi|al-Farabi]] that the virtuous city is ruled by one reference, God and not whims, and that when people's awareness of returning to God is absent the city becomes ignorant, unjust or wicked, and he sees the 'safe homes' as part of this city. This is the lecturer's presentation. (needs_source: the account of Umar and the judgeship and the sayings attributed to al-Farabi have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:03:30}
- He says when values shake each judges by his own whim, so you hear the husband and see that he is the wronged one, and hear the wife and see that she is, because each lies and then believes his lie until he lives in the circle of being the only wronged person. Among lies is for a person to appear of a great family and borrow so people see what he is not, and he says homes are not built on lying and that truthfulness begins with God. See [[concept/kadhib|lying]] and [[concept/al-sidq-truthfulness|truthfulness]]. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:05:00}
- He tells that [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] would weep when reading the verse in which the truthful are asked about their truthfulness ([[passage/quran-33-8|al-Ahzab 8]]), saying (in his words) that if God asks the truthful about their truthfulness how is the liar to be reckoned (his account, without a chain). He mentions that a man keeps on lying and seeking out lying until he is written with God as a liar (in sense), and that a lie is attributed to the liar even if he did not say it because people are used to it from him. He says lying delays work in institutions and halts production, and that a home's comfort and calm and children's disobedience all rest on truth or lying, so father and mother, son and daughter lie, and gives the example of a son asked 'did you pray?' who says yes and has not, because dealing in unsafe homes resembles a policeman's way with a thief or a jailer's with a prisoner. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the saying of al-Hasan al-Basri and the claimed effects of lying on work have no chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:07:00}
- He wants us to deal with our children in friendship, love, affection and a store of goodwill, and likewise with spouses and neighbours, and says when lying spreads in a tribe, a family and a society, the society becomes an empty balloon whose report no one trusts. He suggests that advertising and media companies keep a religious, financial and legal adviser to measure the extent of lying in the media, says if we do not want lying to people then how much less to God, and that taking money without verifying is a kind of lying (the phrase is garbled), and that safe homes are built on honesty in dealing and no betrayal. These are the lecturer's views. (needs_source: the phrase on taking money without verifying is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:08:30}
- On betrayal he mentions the wife of [[person/nuh|Nuh]] and the wife of [[person/lut|Lut]] ([[passage/quran-66-10|al-Tahrim 10]]), explains betrayal as taking the husband's secrets outside the house, and says how many Muslim homes see each side betray the other by showing his faults before people until he is as if naked and his news is on the air. He mentions (in sense) that among the greatest betrayals before God is for a man to confide a matter to his wife and she tells her neighbours of it, and the reverse, and likens homes built on lying to the spider's house ([[passage/quran-29-41|al-Ankabut 41]]) and to the 'clothed yet naked', because their people think they are in a house while naked before people. See [[concept/khiyana|betrayal]]. This is the lecturer's view and interpretation. (needs_source: the reports on divulging a secret and on the clothed yet naked are given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:10:30}
- He says those with resounding slogans often grow used to lying, and tells the story of a man who fled from people who wanted to kill him and hid under a pile of fibre belonging to a rope-maker; when the rope-maker was asked he told the truth that the man was under the pile, and they thought he was lying and left; after the hider came out he seized the rope-maker by the collar in reproach, and he concludes that the liar thinks lying saves him but he is saved neither in this world nor the next. He says (in sense) that scholars say the angel moves away from a servant when he lies because of the stench that came from his mouth. This is the lecturer's story. (needs_source: the story and the scholars' saying about the angel moving away have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:13:30}
- He speaks of double standards in homes far from truthfulness: a husband goes out to work uneasy about his mother and sister at home, and he says if the husband were assured that his wife treated his mother and sister as she treats her own mother and sister the home would be safe, whereas in an unsafe one the man phones every ten minutes to be reassured, and he describes it as having 'white teeth and black hearts'. He says we meet on piety, and if we do not meet on it I lie to you and you lie to me. This is a generalisation of the lecturer's; some phrases are garbled. (needs_source: some phrases are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:16:00}
- He mentions (in sense) the report of the two litigants one of whom may be more eloquent in his plea so the judge rules for him, and that the judge is a human being, then applies it to the family: if your daughter comes complaining of her husband do not side with her at once nor count her an angel, for neither she nor the husband nor we are angels, and wait until her husband comes, and your son-in-law will thank you for it and feel there is an elder with sense in the house. He mentions a saying of the Prophet that none of you should report to him anything about his companions, for he likes to come out to you with a sound heart (in sense). This is the lecturer's advice. (needs_source: both reports are given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:18:30}
- He says lying in deeds is graver than lying in words, and that the safe home is founded on its members' fearing God, so if a wife sees a change from her husband she should accuse herself and look to her relation with God, and if a husband sees that his wife does not respect him he should examine his prayer, his reading of the Qur'an, his charity and alms, his keeping of kinship and his wealth. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:20:00}
- He tells (in sense) of [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]] saying that he knows the sin that makes the character of his wife and mount bad, that it is he who was discourteous with God and so the evils of creation were set on him. He tells the story of a Bedouin on his way to Syria who saw a shepherd with some thirty dogs and was told they were wolves not dogs, which had not eaten the sheep since [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] took office, whereupon Umar wept and said that they set right what was between them and their Lord, so God set right what was between the wolf and the sheep. He concludes that whoever mends what is between him and God, God mends what is between him and his colleagues, wife, neighbour, family, parents and children. These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: the report of Ibn Abbas and the wolves story have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:21:30}
- He says the great calamity is for a person to put people's faults under the microscope and shrink his own, and so uninvolved parties enter, such as the wife's mother and the husband's brother, likening it to a suit brought by one with no standing, and asks that a person not poke his nose into people's lives but mend his own, and not lie to himself or to others. He calls on those who keep voluntary acts yet hurt their spouses or disclose their secrets to review the fruit of their uprightness. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:23:00}
- He says the worst father is one who does not bear his responsibility toward his children, laughing with his colleagues and frowning at home, and that each of us is a shepherd responsible for his flock. He tells that a man asked [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] for a governorship and went in to find him on all fours with his children riding his back, which relieved the man, who then said that when he enters his house the standing stand and the sleeper wakes, and Umar said if you are not merciful to your own children how will you be merciful to the children of Muslims, and counted him as failing. He infers that whoever wrongs his wife destroys his state, and mentions the charge to care for the two weak, the orphan and the woman (in sense). This is the lecturer's story and view. (needs_source: the story of Umar and the applicant and the charge on the two weak have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:25:30}
- He counts it a kind of lying that a woman talks of values and voluntary acts while her husband never sees her teeth, that is, she does not smile, and she complains about everything, and he says complaint should be made to God ([[passage/quran-12-86|Yusuf 86]]). This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:26:30}
- Calls: a Kurdish woman from northern Iraq, living in the Netherlands and married to an Egyptian, says her husband is not honest with her and wants to separate, that a sheikh from the mosque spoke with her, and asks his advice. Some personal details come from garbled text. (needs_source: some personal details are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:28:00}
- Another woman asks two questions: about her sister, who tells her husband false things, and about her husband's mother, who passes on false talk about her son although she sees her husband as truthful, religious and content, and asks the lecturer to advise her sister and her aunt. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:30:00}
- A woman says she has come to know a married man with four children who sometimes lies, and fears he may be hiding the matter from his first wife if he proposes to her. The text is garbled and nothing beyond what is clear is recorded. (needs_source: the text of the call is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:31:30}
- Answering the Kurdish woman he says Kurds are dear because [[person/salah-al-din-yusuf-ibn-ayyub|Saladin]] came from them, that one used to lying finds truth hard as one who has left prayer finds praying hard, and that truth tastes bitter on the tongues of liars like medicine. He advises that she start by reforming him and pray God to make him truthful, not provoke him over separation, and go to the Islamic centre and the imams in the Netherlands so they intervene to reconcile and set a general framework for his relation with his wife. This is the lecturer's view and counsel. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:34:00}
- To the caller from Sharjah he says homes are demolished by lying and strengthened by truth, then says lying is not allowed except in what keeps life going such as reconciling disputants, and that a husband's kind words to his wife and a wife's pleasing words to her husband are permissible lying, whereas forbidden is the lying of a husband who pleads illnesses, is arrogant, shirks responsibility, always complains and frowns, and likewise the deceitful and twisting wife. This is the lecturer's view on a question of jurisprudence; he gave no evidence. (needs_source: the ruling on permissible lying has no evidence stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:35:30}
- He tells the story of a righteous mother whose son fell ill; the father went out with the Prophet to the prayer and the boy died; when the father returned he asked after the boy and she said he had slept as he had never slept, so the father thought he had rested; then before dawn she told him that neighbours had been entrusted with a deposit by some people and when its owners wanted it back they refused, and that their son was a deposit its owner has taken back; and the Prophet prayed for blessing on their night (told in sense without naming them). See [[concept/sabr|patience]] and [[concept/amana|trust]]. This is a story the lecturer tells. (needs_source: the story is given in sense without names or a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:36:30}
- To the second question, on the husband's mother who passes on false talk, he advises her to stop interfering in her children's homes because we are of a different generation from theirs, not to charge children against the other side, and to build up credit with her son's wife that she can draw on at need, and says homes need gentleness, softness and truth, and hopes these questions do not anger the other parties because people take from a scholar a neutral answer. This is the lecturer's counsel. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:38:30}
- Abu Umar asks in which case a wife is told that this husband is unfit and her case hopeless, and a husband told that his wife is unfit. The lecturer then sets out a case of a wife whose husband has left prayer and observance, strikes her, belittles her before her children, is stingy with her and shames her before the neighbours, and says two arbiters from both sides may step in ([[passage/quran-4-35|al-Nisa 35]]), and that if a wife insists on giving up the hijab and leaving prayer and constant complaint and belittling her husband then divorce is unavoidable, but after attempts and patience and bidding the family to pray (the end of the phrase is garbled). See [[concept/talaq|divorce]]. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the end of the phrase is garbled and the question is general) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:43:30}
- Umm Rawan from Sharjah says her husband is not neglectful in his religion but she feels he speaks for his own interest, that she searches his messages though she knows this is spying (see [[passage/quran-49-12|al-Hujurat 12]]), and that her husband sometimes threatens divorce. The lecturer says she has put him under the microscope and questions him like a prosecutor, that the Arab man by nature disdains being interrogated, especially by his wife, and advises her to look for factors of building and not demolition and to look at his good qualities. He gives the example of the child who brings seven excellent subjects and one weak and is blamed for it, and describes the spouse who asks ten things and gets eight and asks after the other two as among the defrauders ([[concept/tatfif-short-measure|short measure]]), citing Pharaoh's saying 'I show you only what I see' ([[passage/quran-40-29|Ghafir 29]]). This is the lecturer's counsel and his generalisation about the Arab man. (needs_source: the generalisation on the nature of the Arab man has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep24 00:45:30}

## Episode 25: the language of dialogue in the home
- He asks whether there is a language of dialogue among family members or whether it has evaporated in the street, school, university and the whole world. He gives as the first cause that we were not accustomed to the manners of dialogue from childhood, so we scold the boy if he interrupts us and interrupt him when he speaks, and calls for listening until the boy, girl, wife or husband finishes, and if someone goes on too long we tell him to put his words in points and state his aim, not in the manner of a policeman with a thief but on one level ground. He says the Prophet was the greatest to converse with humans and jinn and turned his whole face to the one speaking to him (his statement, without a source). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the description of the Prophet as one who conversed with the jinn has no source and the words-per-minute figure is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:03:30}
- He says the husband who holds the newspaper while his wife talks to him is not ordering his priorities, that we should give the speaker our attention even if we find the matter small, like a boy who has a cat or an aquarium that is his life, that we should come down to the level of our children's talk and not wait for them to rise to ours, and that a wife is a female who wants the detail of the details and enters into what lies behind the letters, so the husband should be patient. He tells (in sense) that [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]] said of a servant who wronged him that he was learning patience on him. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the account of Ali and the servant is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:05:30}
- The second cause is pride. He tells (in sense) that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] came in on the Prophet asleep on a mat that had left marks on his side and wept, mentioning Chosroes and Caesar in silk, and that the Prophet said to him are you in doubt, son of al-Khattab, this is prophethood not kingship. He says the realm of the Chosroes and the Caesars is lost but in our homes today there is a Chosroes and a Caesar: the daughter a Chosroes, the mother a Caesar, the father the Caesar of Caesars and the son the Chosroes of Chosroes, because each wants everyone to move to him, and that whim makes deaf and blind. See [[concept/kibr|pride]]. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the account of Umar and the mat is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:08:30}
- Among the causes of losing dialogue are raising the voice and weak argument, and of the cool-nerved husband he says he has feeling but has patience, and that the winds of anger must be absorbed before they blow, mentioning a Bedouin's words to his wife not to peck at him as one pecks a tambourine (in sense, without a source). If the husband is angry and enters the room and closes it, his wife should leave him until he calms and then two words suffice; he is not [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] but a modern man on whom the worries of the age fall, with a devil beside him. Likewise the absence of goodwill credit between those in dialogue. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the Bedouin's words to his wife are given in sense with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:11:00}
- He says we should not look at the black spot but at the whole white garment, and tells (in sense) that [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Isa]] passed the stinking carcass of a dog and the disciples said how foul its smell, and he said how white its teeth. He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] stepped on the foot of a man asleep in the mosque before dawn who said are you blind, and he smiled and said no, and those around him wanted to punish the man but he said he asked me a question and I answered. He says that whoever fears God, people love him even if not convinced by his ideas (ascribing it to our scholars). These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: the stories of Isa and Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz and the saying ascribed to scholars have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:14:00}
- Among the causes of lost dialogue is prior hatred: the boy takes the morsel from his mother's hand holding his breath as if taking poison, and one who tightens up against his wife's relatives before they arrive; he says we live life in order to live it, not to understand its finest details, and one should have a readiness to converse with the other and not make victory the aim. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:15:30}
- He tells that [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]] and [[person/yahya-ibn-main|Yahya ibn Ma'in]] entered a mosque and found a storyteller relating on their authority a fabricated report on the merit of 'there is no god but God', and they told him they were Ahmad and Yahya and had not narrated it, and the man said is there not in the world another Ahmad and Yahya (a story without a chain). He says pride makes its owner reject the truth, and sets down the rule 'accept the truth even from a distant enemy and reject falsehood even from a near beloved'. This is the lecturer's story and rule. (needs_source: the story of Ahmad and Yahya with the storyteller has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:18:00}
- He tells (in sense) that when [[person/saad-ibn-abi-waqqas|Sa'd]] became Muslim his mother refused food and drink until he went back on his religion and he told her that if she had a hundred souls and they went out one by one he would not disbelieve, and concludes that a person has constants and variables, that we are not in a battle, and that the true victory is to overcome our devil and win in the manners of dialogue. This is the lecturer's account, without a chain. (needs_source: the account of Sa'd and his mother is given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:19:00}
- He says a house in which voices rise and people interrupt one another is doubted from the start, since gentleness placed in a home gives it calm, that our problems today are poverty of hearts and tongues and a lack of the kind word, and says (in sense) 'son of Adam, speak good and profit or keep silent and be safe'. A child has a key to his personality: some come by encouragement, others by warning. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the saying 'son of Adam, speak good' is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:20:30}
- He cites the difference of tongues and colours as a sign ([[passage/quran-30-22|al-Rum 22]]) and holds that the difference includes psychological make-up and upbringing. He tells that the mother of [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] named him Atiq so that he might be freed from the Fire (a story without a source). He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] saw in a dream a man of his descendants with a gash on his forehead who would fill the earth with justice, and that Asim son of Umar married the daughter of the milk-seller who bore Layla, who married [[person/abd-al-aziz-ibn-marwan|Abd al-Aziz ibn Marwan]] and bore [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]], whose head was gashed by a horse when he was five (his telling, with garbled text). See [[person/asim-ibn-umar|Asim ibn Umar]]. This is the lecturer's story. (needs_source: the story of Atiq, Umar's dream and the lineage of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz's mother have no source and the text is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:23:00}
- He closes the passage by saying that in the language of dialogue a person should be grateful for God's favours, rely on Him and be truthful in what he says, and if a hard problem presses on him he should seek God's help with two units of prayer, and promises a coming episode on the language of dialogue in the language of the Qur'an. See [[concept/tawakkul|reliance on God]]. This is the lecturer's announcement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:23:30}
- Calls: Abdullah from Switzerland, whose wife is Christian and who have a four-year-old daughter, says she does not drink wine or eat pork and honours his mother, that he takes his daughter to Friday prayer, and sees a lack of dialogue between them and asks advice. Then Nuha from Dubai calls: four years of love and constant dialogue, then it was found that the reason a child was delayed was her husband, and tube treatment failed twice, after which dialogue stopped and he sank into television and the internet and she fears for the relationship. Personal details come from text partly garbled. (needs_source: some personal details are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:25:30}
- Abd al-Hafiz asks about the real causes of the absence of the language of dialogue in Islamic and especially Arab homes, unlike European societies. The lecturer answers that among the causes are no readiness for dialogue, prior backgrounds, entering a battle we want to win, and the passivity of some who were not given the manners of dialogue when small, and says a father should ask his children what they think and not be like the father who orders his wife to silence the children so he can prepare a lecture on consultation and democracy, who ought to go to a psychiatrist. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:37:30}
- Umm Muhammad says she is sharp and her husband calm in raising their three children, and mentions (in sense) 'play with them seven, discipline them seven, befriend them seven', and holds that at a certain age the boy should be his father's companion. The lecturer answers that if both spouses are sharp one of them must calm by prior agreement, and tells (in sense) that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] knew Umar's harshness and prepared gentle words and Umar knew Abu Bakr's harshness and prepared gentle words as they went to the Saqifa, and says a mother's sharpness is often from the husband's absent role and his not embracing her, and that everyone should embrace one another. This is the lecturer's answer. (needs_source: the account of Abu Bakr, Umar and the Saqifa is in sense without a chain and the 'play seven' saying has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:39:00}
- Maryam from Belgium, who is twenty-three, asks how many days of past Ramadans she missed, not knowing the number, and another woman, Moroccan, asks about a marriage with her family's knowledge but without the knowledge of the family of her Emirati suitor, whether it is valid in law and under the Shari'a. To the first the lecturer answers that she should work out when she reached puberty and estimate the days she owes in each Ramadan and then make them up by fasting on Mondays and Thursdays, and to the second he says he does not issue a fatwa on the air and that she should return to the European Council for Fatwa. This is the lecturer's counsel, not a fatwa. (needs_source: the make-up by Mondays and Thursdays is given with no evidence stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:40:00}
- Abd al-Hamid calls from Dubai and speaks of the philosophy of language, the dialogical reason and the need for years to understand the philosophy and manners of dialogue, and the lecturer comments that this is all wisdom. In closing he says we begin with ourselves and wait for the speaker, hopes the language of dialogue in homes will begin in Arabic, our mother tongue, and that no people has insulted its language as the Arabs have (his statement), and that if we are forced in societies to use the people's language there is no need for it in homes. See [[concept/arabiyya|Arabic]]. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:41:00}
- To the caller from Switzerland whose wife is not Muslim, the lecturer says that Islam for her is you, so if she does not find in you the manners of dialogue she will think Islam has none, that if you wrong her it is a religion of wrong, and if you tend and embrace her it is a religion of embrace and mercy, and that you should be her door to Islam with wisdom and open your heart to her so she enters gradually, and prays for her guidance. This is the lecturer's counsel. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:34:30}
- To Nuha he says that in the language of dialogue we should forbid the phrase 'the fault is his' or 'the fault is hers', and points to the saying that He grants females and males or pairs them males and females 'and makes whom He wills barren' ([[passage/quran-42-50|al-Shura 50]]), holding that God may withhold offspring for a better wisdom, that the husband who withdrew to the internet and television because he was not given a child is passive and should be told seek help from God and do not be helpless, and that provision is not liquidity alone but health, marriage and children, and the lack of them a kind of it. He mentions [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]] and [[person/zakariyya|Zakariyya]] and the astonishment of the wife at old age and the angel's saying do you wonder at God's command. This is the lecturer's view and interpretation. (needs_source: the attribution of the angel's saying to Zakariyya's wife is unclear in the text and the age of Ibrahim is given as eighty-six by him) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:36:30}
- Muhammad from Belgium, about to marry, says his fiancée speaks nervously in dialogue, and the lecturer asks whether she is nervous always or on certain matters and whether he himself is patient, then tells him that if he goes mad during the engagement how will it be after marriage, that the engagement is the best period for dialogue, and calls him to seek guidance in prayer and to explain the matter to her, mentioning an Arab saying that if I err quickly and you anger quickly we shall never agree. This is the lecturer's counsel. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:43:30}
- He sums up that we should listen to the person before us and discuss with him rationally without philosophy and without desire to defeat him, for we are not in border negotiations or buying-and-selling bargaining, and if we see a flaw in his speech we alert him politely by stating what he said correctly and then showing what we did not understand. He says many wives complain that their husbands go mute in the home though they were talkative in the engagement, and hopes the language of dialogue returns to homes. This is the lecturer's summary. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep25 00:45:30}

## Episode 26: the language of the Qur'an in the home
- He defines safe homes as those whose members treat one another by the way: the husband's embracing of his wife, the child's honouring of father and mother, the daughter's obedience, the elder brother's tenderness and the young one's respect. He says part of this way is the language of the Qur'an, which is Arabic, and that no people has wronged its language as the Arabs have (his statement), foreign jargon entering Arab homes, and counts being Arab a favour that Muslims across the earth envy because they understand the Qur'an, and holds that we should teach our non-Arab brothers this language. See [[concept/arabiyya|Arabic]]. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:03:00}
- He criticises broadcasters' mistakes: their saying 'the delegation withdrew' (insahaba) because sahb is dragging a thing on the ground and the right word to him is 'left' (kharaja), and their saying 'artificial satellites' (istina'iyya) because istina' is choosing for oneself as in 'and I made you for Myself' ([[passage/quran-20-41|Ta-Ha 41]]) and the right word is 'industrial/man-made' (sina'iyya), and complains of weak composition among pupils. These are the lecturer's linguistic views. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:05:00}
- He faults the facile use of foreign words such as taxi and bus in place of 'hire car' and 'carrier', and the saying of 'hello' on the telephone in place of 'peace be upon you', mentioning that whoever says it gains thirty good deeds (in sense without a chain), and says we are ascetic about good deeds and about our language but not about money. He corrects common mistakes, in his view: 'the effect of smoking on so-and-so's health' with the right preposition, 'I understood from you' not 'on you', 'I contacted you', 'specialist' in its form (ikhtisasi) and not the other (akhissa'i), 'hafada' and not 'ahfad' for grandchildren citing the verse ([[passage/quran-16-72|al-Nahl 72]]), and 'hawi' (amateur) not 'ghawi' because the ghawi is the one gone astray. These are the lecturer's linguistic views. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary and the thirty-good-deeds report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:08:30}
- He tells that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] asked a seller to sell him a garment and he said 'no, may God not keep you well', and Abu Bakr said did you say 'no, and may God keep you well', since 'may God not keep you well' is a prayer against him, and says 'la zala mariDan' is a prayer against the sick man and the right form is 'la yazalu'. This is the lecturer's story and linguistic view. (needs_source: the story of Abu Bakr and the seller has no chain and the lexical claim is unchecked) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:10:30}
- He says the fu'ad is not the qalb: the fu'ad is the space the heart occupies, hence 'the fu'ad of the mother of Musa became empty' ([[passage/quran-28-10|al-Qasas 10]]), while the qalb is the organ itself. This is the lecturer's linguistic view. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:11:30}
- He tells that [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] erred in prayer while reciting the verse on the thief's punishment ([[passage/quran-5-38|al-Ma'ida 38]]) and ended it with 'forgiving, merciful', whereupon a Bedouin corrected him with 'mighty, wise', and when the imam asked him whether he knew the sura he said no but that had it been 'forgiving, merciful' the thief would not have been sentenced to the cutting of his hand. He infers the care of the one who listens to God's speech. This is the lecturer's story, without a chain. (needs_source: the story of Abu Hanifa and the Bedouin has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:13:00}
- He says firar with a kasra is uncovering an animal's teeth to know its age and farar with a fatha is flight as in 'the death from which you flee' ([[passage/quran-62-8|al-Jumu'a 8]]). He wishes a decision would be issued that schools and universities set aside a day for classical Arabic, and holds that memorising the Qur'an when young brings the tongue close to the language. These are the lecturer's views. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:15:00}
- He distinguishes qira'a (of a single word) from tilawa (of words following one another, verse after verse), and says the cup (ka's) is feminine as in 'its mixture is ginger' ([[passage/quran-76-17|al-Insan 17]]) and the well (bi'r) is feminine as in 'an abandoned well and a lofty palace' ([[passage/quran-22-45|al-Hajj 45]]). This is the lecturer's linguistic view. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:16:00}
- He says a child can learn five languages in the first seven years so other languages need not harm the mother tongue, and that France bans shop signs in anything but French and the United Arab Emirates does not allow writing in other than Arabic (his claim). Then he faults 'a collision (suddam) occurred' in place of 'collided (istidam)' because suddam is, to him, an animal disease. These are the lecturer's views. (needs_source: the claim on bans of shop signs in France and the Emirates and the meaning of 'suddam' have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:18:00}
- He tells that [[person/labid-ibn-rabia|Labid]], author of one of the Mu'allaqat, stopped poetry after the Qur'an came down, and that the Prophet asked him where his poetry was and he said poetry no longer ran on his tongue as it had and that he was awed by what is in God's Book (a story without a chain). He distinguishes sadd, which is intentional, from man'. This is the lecturer's story and linguistic view. (needs_source: the story of Labid with the Prophet has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:19:30}
- He explains the difference between 'they could not scale it' and 'they could not pierce it' in the account of the barrier ([[passage/quran-18-97|al-Kahf 97]]) as the added letter ta signalling difficulty because the barrier was of copper, iron and molten brass. He distinguishes da'f (lacking strength to begin with) from wahn (a weakness that strikes the strong) and mentions 'do not weaken' ([[passage/quran-3-139|Al Imran 139]]) and that on the day of Uhud when it was rumoured that the Prophet was killed one of the Companions said why do we not die on what he died on, which roused their zeal and they returned to the fight (told in sense without naming him). This is the lecturer's linguistic view. (needs_source: the account of the Companion at Uhud is in sense without a name or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:22:30}
- He says in 'every year and you are well' the waw signals continuity, and that 'I visited all the countries' with kaffa is not right since kaffa is an adverbial. He mentions that European rulers in the Middle Ages put Arabic words in their speech to show their culture (his claim), and stresses that he does not deny other languages but not at the expense of the mother tongue, and that we converse among ourselves in Arabic unless necessity requires otherwise. These are the lecturer's views. (needs_source: the claim on European rulers and Arabic words has no source and the lexical claims are unchecked) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:25:00}
- He says homes have to do with the language of the Qur'an because they are shaken when they leave the Qur'an and its language, that our children may be wronged by a complicated way of teaching while Arabic is easy if made easy, and calls for classes on Arabic and the Qur'an and poetry and oratory contests, says the tribes celebrated when a poet arose, and mentions (without chains) that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] read poetry and told sound from weak, that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] liked fine speech, that [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]] was a poet and that [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]] has a diwan. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: what he mentions of the Companions, al-Shafi'i and the tribes has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:27:30}
- Fahd from Riyadh says universities in Arab countries teach all subjects in English after the pre-university stage where Arabic is stressed, and that this causes pupils a contradiction unlike France and Germany (his statement), and the lecturer agrees with him and promises to speak on the subject. This is a call. (needs_source: the comparison with universities in other countries has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:29:00}
- Abd al-Aziz from Abu Dhabi, an Arabic teacher, says among common mistakes is repeating 'kullama' and 'bi-qadri ma' in the same sentence, and that giving pupils a long poem makes them attached to the language. Ibrahim from Egypt points to language mistakes on shop signs and cars and hopes municipalities will follow them up. These are calls. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:31:30}
- Na'ima from Algeria, an Arabic teacher, says teachers do not stress sound pronunciation or classical Arabic and calls teachers to jealousy for the language. Umm Marwan from Fujairah suggests pupils prepare posters hung in the classroom such as 'Arabic is the language of science', 'Arabic is the language of the Noble Qur'an' and 'Arabic is the language of religion' to remind them of its value. These are calls. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:33:30}
- A caller asks about two verses that seem alike on the surface, on a soul not being charged beyond its capacity and what it has been given ([[passage/quran-2-286|al-Baqara 286]] and [[passage/quran-65-7|al-Talaq 7]]). No answer is recorded in the passage, as the text falls into repetition. (needs_source: no answer is recorded and the text is repetition) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:34:30}
- He says that when Muslims slept in the light others woke in the dark, so the language of modern science became other than Arabic, and hopes Arabic will not be forgotten in universities, that Arabic be taught in the faculties of medicine, science, engineering and commerce, and that the new generation take up the banner of science so that Arabic is again the language of science. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:36:00}
- Umm Rayyan from France says her husband cannot speak Arabic and so cannot pray or read the Qur'an, and no answer is recorded. Nadhir from Ajman asks about the difference between words such as dhahaba, raha and sara and their opposites, and the lecturer refers him to the programmes of specialists and members of the language academies. These are calls.
