(needs_source: no answer is recorded for Umm Rayyan) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:38:00}
- He sums up what the callers said: an appeal to municipalities and official bodies to check signs, since our language is our identity, Na'ima's point that the matter lies in schools and the media as well, and that father and mother should not speak different languages before the children except by necessity and that we should alert a son or daughter who uses foreign jargon at home. This is the lecturer's summary. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:41:00}
- He adds corrections: say 'nasrani' not 'masihi' because God named them Nasara, and holds that a Muslim's Islam is not complete without belief in [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Isa]] and the Gospel sent down to him, that 'hubut' is not 'nuzul' ([[passage/quran-2-61|al-Baqara 61]]), that 'nasa'im' is the plural of 'nasim', that 'wasita' is the jewel in a necklace and 'wasata' is mediation to reconcile, that 'mamnun' is not 'shakir', and that the plural is 'al-mudirun' not 'mudara'. These are the lecturer's linguistic views; what touches religion is his opinion. (needs_source: the lexical claims have not been checked against a dictionary) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep26 00:44:30}

## Episode 27: the character of muru'a in Islam
- He wonders at the small fruit of lessons of knowledge, sermons and satellite channels, and sees the cause in behavioural handicaps: the arrogant, the vain, the show-off, the liar and the two-faced who meets you smiling then slanders you. He mentions that [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]] saw [[person/musa|Musa]] as a corrupter in the land ([[passage/quran-40-26|Ghafir 26]]) and that the people of [[person/lut|Lut]] said of Lut and his household that they are people who keep themselves pure ([[passage/quran-27-56|al-Naml 56]]), so the corrupt person throws his faults on you, which the psychologists call projection. This is the lecturer's interpretation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:00}
- He says the soul has three callers: one to the character of Satan, one to the conduct of the beast and one to the character of the angel. One in whom pride, envy, hatred, rancour, harm and wrong are joined is a human devil, and the human devil takes precedence over the jinn devil because one seeks refuge in God from the jinn devil, whereas the human devil may be from kin, a child, a father or a mother. He mentions the proud and vain who says I am the candle that burns to light others. This is the lecturer's classification. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:00}
- He calls on a person not to count himself as having favour over people but to thank them for leaving him room to do good. He tells that [[person/aisha|Aisha]], when a poor man carried her charity and said God reward you, would say rather may God reward you, since he carried her provision to the hereafter. He tells (in sense) the report of the sheep that was distributed with only the foreleg left, and the Prophet said rather all of it remained and the foreleg went, and that a person has of his wealth only what he ate and used up, wore and wore out, or gave in charity and so kept. These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:00}
- He explains 'a soul will know what it sent ahead and what it left behind' ([[passage/quran-82-5|al-Infitar 5]]): what a person sent ahead is his prayer, alms, pilgrimage and fasting, and what he left behind is a righteous child who prays for him and continuing charity ([[concept/sadaqa-jariya|continuing charity]]) such as building a mosque, a copy of the Qur'an, digging a well, and an endowment for orphans, widows and students of knowledge. He ascribes to our scholars that one who does not pray for his father and mother after every prayer, living or dead, has been disobedient to them (his claim). He says the believer's traces remain in the place of his prostration, and that when the wicked die people are relieved of them (in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the ruling on praying for parents ascribed to scholars and the reports on traces have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:00}
- He observes that people say the former minister and the earlier official but do not say Abu Bakr formerly, nor Abu Hanifa, Malik or Ibn Taymiyya, but say Abu Hanifa says in the present tense though he died in 150 of the Hijra, so the people of knowledge live on in their memory, and says those who lived for religion, God has not let their memory die, and how many millions pray for Abu Bakr and Umar. See [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] and [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]. This is the lecturer's observation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:00}
- He warns of 'raids', the first being the raid of the angel of death, and says some people have a defect in belief: they say God provides and then say so-and-so cut off my provision, whereas the believer does not say that because only God cuts provision, through death, and the martyrs' provision is not cut off ([[passage/quran-9-111|al-Tawba 111]]). This is the lecturer's view on belief. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:00}
- He takes up the character of muru'a (manly virtue): when it evaporates from homes scowling faces appear, and whoever follows the caller of Satan or the beast comes to hate people and be hated. He gives the image of the bee that settles only on flowers, mentions the saying of [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]], be among people like a tree they throw stones at and it throws them fruit (without a chain), and cites the saying to repel evil with what is better so that the one between you and whom there was enmity becomes a close friend ([[passage/quran-41-34|Fussilat 34]]) and what follows ([[passage/quran-41-35|35]] and [[passage/quran-41-36|36]]) on seeking refuge from Satan's prompting. He says doing good is not for the good-doer alone, and that keeping kinship is not by returning like for like (given in sense). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the saying of al-Hasan al-Basri and the saying on keeping kinship have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:00}
- He says that keeping ties is not only to visit those who visit you but to keep ties with the one who cut you off, and that some spouses and children encourage evil and say do not visit your proud uncle, while He has said that among your spouses and children are enemies to you so beware of them ([[passage/quran-64-14|al-Taghabun 14]]), and counts the husband with no protective jealousy as one not trusted with what is sacred. He tells that a man asked for a slave girl of [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]] in marriage and was told she has a fault, that she looks at men, and the suitor said what fault is that, so Ibn Abbas refused him. This is the lecturer's view and story. (needs_source: the story of Ibn Abbas and the suitor has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:00}
- He tells the account of the Prophet's proposal to [[person/aisha|Aisha]]: a woman came to Abu Bakr and did not find him, and told [[person/umm-ruman|Umm Ruman]], who said wait until Abu Bakr comes; he had promised her to the son of al-Mut'im ibn Adi, so he went to al-Mut'im, whose wife said we fear you will turn our son from his fathers' religion, and Abu Bakr said to al-Mut'im I am speaking to you, what has it to do with you, whereupon the promise was lifted and the Prophet proposed and married her. He infers that guardianship is not by force and abuse but by embracing and example, and that the absence of the real role of man and woman is what breaks homes apart. This is the lecturer's story and view. (needs_source: the account of the proposal to Aisha is given in his telling without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:00}
- He returns to the caller of the angel: the angel is good-doing, dutiful and obedient, nothing leaves his tongue but good, glorification is to him as breathing is to us, and he neither eats, drinks nor sleeps. He mentions that the Prophet was a human being ([[passage/quran-18-110|al-Kahf 110]]) who is pleased and angry, sleeps, eats and marries. Then he says an institution without love between its head and its workers is like graves faced with marble outside and worms within, and that favouring the applauders and shunning those who advise is not the muru'a of a Muslim. These are the lecturer's views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:00}
- He takes up the muru'a of the limbs, first the tongue with good words. He tells (in sense) that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] said he loved the world only for three things, among them sitting with people who pick the best speech as the best fruit is picked. He says son of Adam, speak good and profit or be silent and be safe, that a tongue's wound is worse than the spear's, and that you own a word while you have not uttered it and once uttered it owns you. He repeats that the angel moves two miles from the liar for the stench of what left his mouth, that God veils sins and they have no smell, and that reckoning is in the hands of the Forbearing. These are the lecturer's view and stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:00}
- He says the muru'a of character is to be just with those you love and those you do not ([[passage/quran-5-8|al-Ma'ida 8]]), to accept the truth from whoever brings it, to look at what was said and not at who said it, and that wisdom is the believer's lost property (in sense). He says the Arab by nature is emotional and quick to feeling and finds fault with people and time but not with himself. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. (needs_source: the report on wisdom as the believer's lost property is in sense without a chain and the generalisation on the Arab has no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:00}
- He gives the example of one who places a magnifying lens on people's faults and a reducing lens on his own, so he sees his brother's fault as a kilometre and his own as a millimetre, and here the tree of pride grows, and mentions the employee who thinks the institution rests on him. He tells (in sense) that when God loves a servant He calls [[person/jibril|Jibril]] to love him, who calls among the angels, and acceptance is laid down for him on earth. This is the lecturer's illustration. (needs_source: the report on Jibril's love is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:00}
- He tells that [[person/ali-zayn-al-abidin|Ali Zayn al-Abidin]] was at the circumambulation by the time of Hisham ibn Abd al-Malik and the people made way for him, and that a man who did not know him insulted him and called him showy, wicked and hypocritical, and he answered God reward you with good, then said you reminded me of three qualities and I have more of them, would you like me to list them, and the man said I testify that you are the son of the Messenger of God. He mentions that [[person/salman-al-farisi|Salman al-Farisi]] used to say I am the son of Islam, and that a young man in the time of [[person/al-hajjaj-ibn-yusuf|al-Hajjaj]] said I am the son of the man before whom God made the angels prostrate ([[person/adam|Adam]]). These are the lecturer's stories. (needs_source: reports and stories given in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00}
- He complains that the boy cannot write two lines without errors of language and grammar, and says homes have not become safe because we did not secure our language in them, and cites verses of [[person/hafiz-ibrahim|Hafiz Ibrahim]] in the voice of the Arabic language, which are not reproduced here. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00}
- He takes up the muru'a of wealth: that it be earned lawfully, spent lawfully and used to be generous to people, while the miser hates himself, his wife hates him, his children tire of him and he lives the life of the poor and is reckoned as the rich. He relates that [[person/yaqub|Yaqub]] with the shirt of [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] wanted to borrow a needle from a miser to stitch it (a story without a chain). He mentions the muru'a of standing: that a person spend his standing on good intercession, for as wealth has alms so do standing, knowledge and office. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the story of Yaqub and the needle has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:00}
- He mentions that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] on cold days sat in his house with only what covers his nakedness to share the poor's cold, and that [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] used to say he was fasting when food was served to him while he was over Egypt's treasuries in the famine, for fear he would forget the hungry (two stories without chains). He criticises insistence on stockpiling food in freezers as if a famine were coming, and calls for planning while leaving the matter to God ([[passage/quran-96-6|al-Alaq 6]] and [[passage/quran-96-7|7]]). This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the stories of Umar and Yusuf have no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:00}
- He mentions the muru'a of leaving: leaving quarrels, since some brothers spend ten or twenty years in the courts over a small plot and spend on lawyers more than its price, leaving harsh blame, since the Arabs are good at attack and not at gentle reproach, and leaving pressing in demands, like asking for a glass of tea twenty times. He ends with the prayer that the character of muru'a return to homes so that souls grow calm. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep27 00:43:00}

## Episode 28: noses in the home and the rights of mother and wife
- He says the episode may be thorny because it concerns the noses put into homes from outside, especially by the husband's mother or the wife's mother in new homes, and the mixing of rights and duties in the husband between his mother and his wife. He holds that the woman's role in civilisation is stronger and loftier than the man's, since man developed material civilisation and the righteous woman preserved the civilisation of values by raising the children. This is the lecturer's view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:00}
- He asks why happiness evaporated from our homes despite the washing machine, the vacuum cleaner and the oven, complaining that women suffer gout, brittle bones and back pain while the grandparents' homes were better off with few means, and says the husband's mother was closer to her son's wife than to her son and defended her against him (his claim). He holds that God's Book has not changed and rights are still clear, but the concept of happiness has changed, and we look at the symptom and do not seek the disease. This is the lecturer's view. (needs_source: the comparison between grandparents' homes and today's and the claim about the husband's mother have no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:00}
- He says the marriage contract gives the right of sharing and not the right of domination, that Arabs love control and holding the initiative, and wants husbands and wives with psychological health who accept the other with faults, and says hundreds of problems come to him from relatives on both sides putting their noses into the family's life. He holds that the happiness of the husband's mother is to see her son master of his house and his wife obedient, and the wife's is that her husband be hers and not his family's, each measuring by her own scale. This is the lecturer's view and generalisation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:00}
- He tells (in sense) that [[person/aisha|Aisha]] felt jealous when she learned of the Prophet's marriage to the beautiful [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]] and that [[person/hafsa|Hafsa]] eased it for her by saying she was old, and that when she saw [[person/safiyya-bint-huyayy|Safiyya]] she said I saw nothing but a Jewess, and infers that jealousy between women is natural and that justice in pleasure and anger is required ([[passage/quran-5-8|al-Ma'ida 8]]), and asks mothers and sisters to intervene only within limits and a mother not to look at her son as the child who was on her shoulder. These are the lecturer's stories and view. (needs_source: the account of Aisha's jealousy of Umm Salama and Safiyya is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:30}
- He says God commanded companionship in kindness with the mother ([[passage/quran-31-15|Luqman 15]]) and living with the wife in kindness ([[passage/quran-4-19|al-Nisa 19]]), and that putting them in two pans of a scale is wrong to both since each has a different kind of rights, likening it to comparing pens with towers. He warns the husband that in a moment of closeness he complains of his family to his wife and then things change, and says (in sense) love your beloved moderately and hate your hated moderately. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the saying 'love your beloved moderately' is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:30}
- He asks the husband not to carry his irritability over to his wife, since his father and mother put up with him because he is part of them, but his wife is not part of him, and the wife not to be lazy and sleep till mid-morning with an employed husband nor treat him as a policeman treats a thief, and that obedience is in what is right. He says doing good to the wife is doing good in the face of bad manners, foulness of tongue and stinginess, and that the Prophet took the opinion of [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]] (in sense). This is the lecturer's counsel. (needs_source: the account of the Prophet taking Umm Salama's opinion is in sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:00}
- He gives three counsels: to the relatives of both sides not to put their noses into their sons' and daughters' homes, to the wife's mother to teach her daughter that her loyalty after marriage is to her husband, and to the husband not to stop his wife from honouring her family and keeping ties, even with a weekly visit. He says visit seldom and love grows (a current saying) except for a mother who has only this son ([[passage/quran-17-23|al-Isra 23]]). To the wife he says to count the husband's mother as a mother and bear with her, then please her husband in her, and God will be pleased with her and she will find someone to care for her in old age. See [[practice/birr-al-walidayn|honouring parents]]. This is the lecturer's counsel. (needs_source: the saying 'visit seldom' has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:00}
- He says commanding and forbidding with no logic does not work, so it is wrong for a husband to say you will never visit your mother, and whoever wants to be obeyed should command what can be done, homes not being military barracks. He warns against nosiness about what a husband bought and why he bought for his wife and not his sister, and says (his view) that the husband's poor brother and sister are among the first to receive zakat from him while his mother, whom he must maintain, does not receive it ([[concept/zakat|zakat]]), and that souls are disposed to love one who does them good (in sense). He calls for seeking God's help, praying two units, and not carrying tomorrow's worry. This is the lecturer's guidance. (needs_source: the ruling on giving zakat to siblings and not the mother has no evidence stated and the saying on disposition has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:30}
- Calls: a woman from Libya complains that her husband, forty-five, speaks with other girls and claims one of them he teaches the Qur'an; Su'ad from Germany, divorced for more than ten years, says her former husband's mother died angry with her and with him since he did not reconcile her, and asks whether what befell her of displacement was because of her husband's disobedience to his mother; Umm Zayd says her mother-in-law treats her husband like a child and he puts his mother and sisters before her and says I will not bring you anything before my mother, and asks about obeying her husband if he asks her to take a loan she does not want. Personal details come from text partly garbled. (needs_source: the text of the calls is garbled and the question on the loan is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:00}
- Abd al-Salam from Libya suggests that spouses look at life through the verse on affection and mercy between spouses ([[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum 21]]) in how each treats the other's mother, and the lecturer thanks him and prays for him. Then Umm Abdullah from al-Ain complains that her husband suddenly changed his mind and wants to return to his own country after they agreed to settle, and a caller from Dubai describes a husband who prays, fasts and has performed the pilgrimage but whose tongue hurts her and who thinks himself better than she, so that she feels she deals with a small boy. No answer is recorded for the last in this passage. (needs_source: no answer is recorded for the caller from Dubai) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:34:00}
- Rashida from Spain says she has been married thirteen years and something befell her in pregnancy or childbearing whose cause she does not know, and asks whether it is a punishment from God, and Umm Fatima from Dubai mentions her daughter who has epilepsy and asks for prayer for healing, and the lecturer prays for her and advises (in an unclear form) the recitation of Surat al-Baqara. The text is garbled and nothing beyond what is clear is recorded. (needs_source: the text of both calls is garbled and the form of the advice is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:30}
- To the caller from Libya whose husband speaks with other girls he advises that she pray for him in every prayer that God protect him from the human and jinn devil, that she look at her own shortcomings, and that she take his good qualities and develop and enlarge them, and not call him to account night and day over small and large matters like a school principal with a pupil. This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:30}
- To Su'ad he says disobedience to parents is a great calamity, expressing a point about its consequence in wording that is garbled in the text, and that the son should keep his mother's ties and pray for her in every prayer, living or dead, perhaps he may be dutiful to her after her death. Then he appeals to rulers and embassies to care for migrant widows and divorced women and return them to their families. See [[concept/uquq-al-walidayn|disobedience to parents]]. This is the lecturer's answer; the statement on the disobedient one's fate is garbled. (needs_source: the statement on the disobedient one's fate is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:30}
- To Umm Muhammad, who complains that her mother-in-law pampers her son and daughters more than she pampers his wife, he says one should look at priorities since a woman who has passed sixty or seventy does not need what a wife of thirty needs, that whoever is disobedient to his mother and father will be disobedient to her, and that what he calls marital flattery, pampering him a little, brings him back to her, and recalls the verse of affection and mercy ([[passage/quran-30-21|al-Rum 21]]). This is the lecturer's answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:30}
- To Umm Abdullah from al-Ain he says the wife should try to persuade her husband to stay if she sees it is better, and if he is not convinced she should follow her husband, and that this should be by understanding and not by commands, prohibitions or unilateral decisions, since the contract gives the right of sharing and not domination. This is the lecturer's view on a disputed question; he gave no evidence. (needs_source: the ruling that the wife follows her husband has no evidence stated) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:30}
- Umm Muhammad from Bahrain says that after twenty-eight years of marriage she was divorced and her children scattered, and she lives with her daughters in Bahrain while her husband and his family do not ask after them, and she has no home or shelter in her own country, and the lecturer tells her that twenty-eight years are not lost with God, that the problem lies with the father and not with her, and prays that she be granted success. Private personal details are mentioned briefly. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:30}
- Umm Fatima says she has been married four years and has two girls, that her husband is extremely irritable, insults, and raises his hand to her and the two little ones, and will not agree to see a psychiatrist because he sees the illness in her. The lecturer says this is projection, a person placing his illness on another, and advises her to pray often in prostration that God guide him. He mentions that the 'black box' is the box of painful memories each person carries, which empties its feelings in the house and fills it with gloom, and calls on Islamic states for an urgent fund for widows and divorced women. This is the lecturer's answer and interpretation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy albuyout-alamena/ep28 00:48:30}
